Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, well, I guess what brings me in is... it's probably just a little misunderstanding at work, really. I just want to do a good job, you know? 

I'm a personal care aide, and we had this staff meeting recently. I guess I just got a little too worked up about safety protocols. I started acting out this whole fall-prevention drill for everybody. I was moving chairs around and waving my arms to show them exactly how to catch someone, just trying to be helpful. But, um... I guess I couldn't seem to stop doing it, even when folks told me to sit down. I just got really carried away. It was a bit embarrassing, honestly, but I'm totally fine now. 

Is it okay that I'm starting with that? I hope I'm answering your question right.

Um... gosh. I really don't want you to think I'm losing my mind or anything like that. I definitely don't see things, I promise. But... well, I guess maybe just a little bit with the hearing part? 

Sometimes when I'm all by myself in the hallway at the center, or... or even just at home down by the laundry room, I'll hear my name. Or it sounds exactly like one of the residents calling out for help, just clear as day. I always go check right away, because I want to make sure everyone is safe, but... there isn't anybody there when I look. 

I figure it's just because I'm so dedicated to my job, you know? Like my ears are just playing tricks on me because I'm always on high alert, listening out for the folks who might need me. It doesn't mean I'm crazy or anything. That doesn't make me sound too weird, does it? I just want to be totally honest with you, but I hope that's a normal thing when you're just trying really hard to be a good caregiver.

I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Has something like that happened to you before?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't really think so. Um... well, no, not like plotting against me. I know that sounds crazy, and I'm definitely not crazy. I just... I do worry sometimes about work, but I think that's normal, right?

Like my ears are just playing tricks on me because I'm always on high alert, listening out for the folks who might need me. It doesn't mean I'm crazy or anything. That doesn't make me sound too weird, does it? I just want to be totally honest with you, but I hope that's a normal thing when you're just trying really hard to be a good caregiver.\n\nDoctor: Are you still hearing your name or the residents calling out for help when you're alone?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I am. I really hope that doesn't mean I'm getting worse, I promise I try my best to just ignore it when it happens. If I'm being completely honest—and I really want to make sure I'm doing a good job answering your questions—it's actually been going on for a little while now. \n\nI'd say... maybe about nine months or so? The hearing the calls that aren't there, and... well, I guess also having some suspicious thoughts, too. Just kind of worrying and wondering about things a bit too much. I know nine months probably sounds like a long time, and I'm really sorry I didn't mention it sooner. I just didn't want you to think I was, you know, totally losing my grip or being a bad patient. \n\nIt's just my mind playing tricks because I want to be a good caregiver, right? I try really hard to make sure I'm being clear when I talk to people. \n\nI guess sometimes my mind gets a little busy, you know, with wanting to make sure I'm doing a good job and all... but I don't think my thoughts are disorganized or anything like that. I'm trying my best to be clear, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there's any truth to the voices you're hearing or the thoughts that someone might be calling for help when no one is there?\n\nPatient: Um... well, with the voices, I really do think it's just my ears playing tricks on me, like I said. Um... no, not really. I don't think strangers are watching me or anything like that. That would be a bit strange, wouldn't it?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I guess sometimes I might get a little ahead of myself when I'm talking. But it's just because I have a lot of good things going on, you know? 

Like, my mind will jump from my job with the seniors, and then to church, and then to my wife, and then I get these really great ideas for a new workshop... and it just goes so fast. Sometimes I lose the first thought before I can even say it right. 

But that just means I'm enthusiastic, right? It's not like I can't hold a conversation. I just have a lot of passion for the things in my life. People like it when you have a lot to say, don't they? I try really hard to be a good conversationalist. Is it okay that my mind works a little fast sometimes?

I'm trying my best to be clear, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there's any truth to the voices you're hearing or the thoughts that someone might be calling for help when no one is there?\n\nPatient: Um... well, with the voices, I really do think it's just my ears playing tricks on me, like I said. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I wouldn't say "special meaning," exactly. That makes it sound a little strange, doesn't it? I just... I try to be really observant, especially at work. 

I mean, I love my job working with the seniors, and I want to do a good job. But sometimes... even when I'm feeling totally calm and fine, I just kind of notice things. Like, I get this feeling that my supervisor and a couple of the others are... well, maybe keeping notes on me? 

It's just little things they say, you know? Little comments here and there. It feels like they're testing me, maybe trying to prove I'm not safe to be around the clients. But I am safe! I'm very careful. I just... I pick up on those little hints they drop. 

It's normal to be aware of your surroundings at work, right? It just means I'm paying attention to my performance. I really want them to see that I'm doing a good job. Is it okay that I notice those kinds of things?
